Spectral method idea:
Given a (nonnegative), compute its zero-padded DFT A. The convolution C = IFFT(A^2). We want C low and flat. We can modify A^2 in frequency: We can choose a new sequence b by setting B such that IFFT(B^2) is flat-ish? Or iterative:
- Let A = DFT(a).
- We want A^2 to have time IFFT flat. We can project C to a target lower envelope? For objective min max, we can use gradient descent in log a enough.

Perhaps we can use "Welch" algorithm: Since C = a*a, to flatten we can adjust spectral phase of A? If we set B(z) = sqrt(C_target(z)) times phase. Need B inverse real nonnegative support. We can choose phase via spectral factorization to make B nonnegative? For a given C polynomial (real nonnegative), we can find causal inverse square root B = sqrt(C(z)) one-sided via cepstrum. If C continuous positive, B may be complex; real sequence B? For C with zero phase? Let's revisit: C(z) = A(z)^2. If we want C with flat time, we can define C polynomial, then choose its one-sided square root via log/cepstrum: B_k = 1/4 IFFT(...). The standard causal spectral factor of a real even nonnegative polynomial. If C is real and nonnegative on unit circle and even, there is a causal min-phase B such that B B(z^{-1})=C? But we need same sequence not reversed: A(z)^2 not A(z)A(z^{-1}). For linear convolution same orientation, A^2, not autocorrelation. If we take arbitrary phase A, its square is C. To get nonnegative time A, need phase such that inverse is nonnegative. This is like finding a real infinite series whose square has prescribed positive coefficients. This is "conformal mapping" maybe.

Can we compute g via a simple fixed-point? Primal: maximize 1^T g, constraints H g ≤ M, g≥0. Dual: minimize M 1^T y, constraints H^T y ≥ 1, y≥0, where H_{k,j}=f_{k-j} if 0≤k-j<n. Let y_k. If we want normalize sum g=1, we can solve minimize t = max_k (f*g)_k over g simplex. This is a zero-sum game as above. The game payoffs: row k (lag), column j (position), payoff H_{k,j}. Row player chooses distribution y over lags, column player g over positions; value v = max_y min_j ∑_k y_k H_{k,j}. Since should be positive. We can find v and optimal strategies with multiplicative weights! The dual feasible y for t? If t=1, normalize? Actually f*g constraints ≤M; scale y: If y is probability, t constraint value v_M? Let's set H normalized? For fixed f, let M=max f*f, and normalize g sum=1. We want min max (f*g). Let v = min_{g simplex} max_k H_{k,j}g_j. By minimax v = max_{y simplex} min_j (H^T y)_j. def fft_conv_self(a):
    n=a.size; size=1<<(2*n-2).bit_length? Need length 2n-1. (2*n-2).bit_length gives next pow2? Example size=1<<(L-1).bit_length()

    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```

```
def fft_convolve(a,b):
    n=a.size; m=b.size; L=n+m-1; size=1<<(L-1).bit_length(); f=np.fft.rfft(a,size); g=np.fft.rfft(b,size); c=irfft(f*g,size); return c[:L]
```

    L=len(a)*2-1
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    c=fft_convolve(a,w_rev)

Peak weights from c:
```
def peak_weights(c, beta, gamma=1.0):
    # use c normalized by max or scale
    cmax=c.max()
    if cmax<=0: return uniform
    # To avoid under/over, compute z = beta * (c - cmax)/cmax? Use softmax on top peaks only.
    # We want weights on near-max. Use z=beta*c/cmax, then subtract max.
    z = beta * c / cmax
    z -= z.max()
    w=np.exp(z)
    s=w.sum()
    if s tiny: uniform on top 10?
```

```
def self_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    size = 1 << (2*n - 2).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
But `irfft` normalizes by 1/N, correct. The self convolution length 2n-1. For n=1, size? 2n-2=0 bit_length 0 ->1<<0=1, works? But n large.

Weight generation:
```
def make_weights(c, q, top_frac=None, floor=0.0):
    cmax = c.max()
    if cmax <=0: return np.ones(len(c))/len(c)
    r = c / cmax
    if q <= 0: return np.full_like(c,1/len(c))? q positive.
    w = r**q
    # Optional: mask smallest lags to zero if q large
    s=w.sum()
    if not finite or s<=1e-300:
        # put on top 5
        w=np.zeros(len(c)); idx=np.argsort(c)[-5:]; w[idx]=1
        s=1
    w /= s
    return w
```
If q=100, r^100 underflows for r<0.8; good. Use q schedule. Could also use rank weights: top K inverse rank to ensure active set? Maybe combine:
```
def rank_weights(c, K, temp=1.0):

    w[vals>K?]=? Not.
```

def log_mirror_optimize(a0, n, budget, verbose=False, name=''):
    a = normalize(a0)
    c = self_conv(a); obj = 2*n*c.max(); best=...
    theta = np.log(a) # allow negative inf? add eps
    start=now
    no_improve=0
    iter=0
    while time < end:
       progress=...
       q = interp(q0,q1,progress) maybe 2 -> 200
       c = self_conv(a)
       w = make_weights(c,q)
       d = gradient_from_weights(w,a)
       # robust normalize
       d_med = np.median(d); d_scale = max(np.percentile(d,99)-np.percentile(d,10), d_med*0.1,1e-12)
       z = (d - d_med) / d_scale
       # clip z to [-10,10]
       z=np.clip(z,-10,10)
       z -= z.mean()
       eta = eta0*(1-progress)**? maybe 0.2 ->0.01
       improved=False
       for m in range(10):
          a_trial = a * np.exp(-eta*z)
          a_trial = normalize(a_trial)
          # Optional if all zero
          c_trial = self_conv(a_trial)
          obj_trial = 2*n*c_trial.max()
          if obj_trial < best? or <obj - tol:
             a=a_trial; obj=obj_trial; improved=True; break
          eta *=0.5
       if not improved:
          # try small noise/restart
          a = normalize(a + noise_scale*rng.normal(n))
       update best
       no_improve...
    return best
```

Now, low-dimensional param optimization:
We can implement `make_power_sequence(n, alpha, beta, gamma)` for p_i ∝ (i+ε)^-α ((n-1-i)+ε)^-β, maybe plus center? For one-sided, beta=0, gamma? Use stable log.
```
def param_sequence(n, alpha=0, beta=0, shift=1, gamma=0, phi=0):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    f=np.log(i+shift) * (-alpha) + np.log(n-1-i+shift)*(-beta) + gamma*(i/n) + phi*np.sin(2π i/n)
    f -= f.max(); p=np.exp(f); p/=p.sum(); return p
```
But optimum may have log shape not polynomial. Scan α 0.0-0.6, β maybe 0-0.4 if want right singular? Since reversal symmetric, α and β cover. Also add linear term gamma to tilt? Actually log p = -α log(i+1) - β log(n-i+1)+γ i/n. We can scan γ [-2,2]. This may produce better. Evaluate for n=4096/8192. Since pow with log stable.

Thus candidate sequence generator:
```
def make_seq(n, alpha, beta, gamma=0.0, left_eps=1.0, right_eps=0.5):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    logp = -alpha*np.log(i+left_eps) + beta*(np.log(n-1-i+right_eps) - np.log(n)) + gamma*(i/(n-1))
    # also maybe add linear in distance to reduce? gamma tilt
    logp -= logp.max()
    p=np.exp(logp); p/=p.sum(); return p
```

Let's outline the final code:
```
import numpy as np, time, math
# no evaluate_sequence import.

def _self_conv(a): ...
def _obj(p): ... returns 2n max (p normalized sum1)
def _normalize(p): p = np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s<1e-300 return ones; return p/s
def _normalize_from_log(l): ...
def _gradient(w,a): ... exact
def _weights_from_c(c,q,top_k=0): power weights
def _param_seq(n, alpha, beta, epsL=1.0, epsR=0.5, gamma=0.0): ...
def _scan_params(n, budget): grid α,β, maybe γ; return best p,obj
```

Local optimizer implementation details:
`adam_log`:
```
def adam_log(a0, n, budget, q0=2.0, q1=100.0, lr0=0.05, lr1=0.005, name=''):
    a=np.maximum(a0,0); a=_normalize(a)
    loga=np.log(a + 1e-300)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    best=a.copy(); best_obj=calc
    start=now
    while now<start+budget:
       t+=1
       progress=...
       q = q0 * (q1/q0)**progress? or linear.
       a=_normalize_from_log(loga)
       c=self_conv(a); obj2n=2*n*c.max(); obj_coeff=c.max()
       update best
       w=power_weights(c,q)
       d=gradient(w,a)
       dot=np.dot(a,d)
       g=a*(d-dot)
       # scale g? Adam adapts.
       m=0.9*m+0.1*g; v=0.99*v+0.01*g*g
       mhat=m/(1-0.9**t); vhat=v/(1-0.99**t)
       lr=lr0*(1-progress)+lr1*progress
       loga -= lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
       # optional if loga range too large, rescale: loga -= loga.mean()
    return best,best_obj
```
At high q, weights on peaks; gradient can be large. Adam okay. It may drift to too skewed; line search not. We can periodically check and if obj increases, shrink lr? Maybe implement simple: maintain best; if current obj > best*(1+1e-3) for many, reduce lr? Time.
For deterministic, same as previous.

`mirror_log`:
```
def mirror_log(a0,budget,q0, q1, eta0, eta1, ):
 loga=log(a)
 while:
   a=normalize_from_log(loga)
   c,obj update
   w=...
   d=gradient(w,a)
   d_mean=d.mean()
   # robust z = (d - d_mean)/(std+eps); clip -6,6
   # Maybe use weights d/?
   z = (d - d_mean); robust=(np.percentile(z,95)-np.percentile(z,5)); if small use mean
   z = z/robust; z=np.clip(z,-4,4); z -= z.mean()
   eta = eta0*(1-progress)+eta1*progress
   # line search
   for back in range(12):
      loga_trial=loga - eta*z
      a_trial=normalize_from_log(loga_trial)
      c_trial=self_conv(a_trial); obj_trial=...
      if obj_trial<obj-1e-12:
         loga=loga_trial; a=a_trial; obj=obj_trial; break
      eta*=0.5
   else:
      # noise/restart
      if no_improve: loga = loga + small noise normalize? 
      eta*=0.25
 return best
```
The z mean subtract before normalization not necessary; shift handles. But subtracting mean helps step size. If d has large outliers, clip.
Use q low for mirror to flatten broad peaks; q0=1.0, q1=50 maybe.

Convex LP-like update:

def convex_mimic(a0, budget, M_factor=1.0, q? steps):
  f=normalize(a0); bestf
  # run a few inner cycles: g=f
  T=10
  for cycle in range(3):
     g=f.copy()
     M = c(f).max() * M_factor
     # If M too small? M_factor 1.0 maybe feasible? g=f feasible (self max=M). So at least f feasible.
     for it in range(steps):
       r=conv(f,g) # f*g
       viol=np.maximum(r-M,0)
       if viol.sum()<1e-15: break
       w=viol/viol.sum()
       d=gradient(w,f)  # derivative wrt g? Wait d_j=∑w_k f_{k-j}; gradient function returns 2∑ f_j w_{i+j}. That's same index? We need d for g_j =∑ w_k f_{k-j} =∑ f_m w_{j+m}. gradient(w,f)/2 returns ∑ f_m w_{i+m}. Yes pass weights w, a=f -> d_j =? gradient(w,f) = 2∑_m f_m w_{j+m}. That's exactly d_j. Good.
       # update g_j *= exp(-eta*d)
       d_scaled=d/(d.max()+eps)
       g *= exp(-0.5*d_scaled) # if log, use logg
       g=normalize(g)
     # mix f with g
     for alpha in [0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]: h=(1-alpha)f+alpha g; if obj(h)<best: f=h
```
But f*g convolution: f length n, g length n. We have `_convolve_ab(f,g)`. Need implement cross convolve. Could use fft_convolve(f,g) size 2n-1 (if both n). We'll implement generic `_convolve(a,b)` with size pow2 >= len(a)+len(b)-1. For f*g length 2n-1.
This update may improve. Use logg to avoid underflow. Steps maybe 50 per cycle. Or run as function for 30s.
Maybe use it to generate g from current best; if g self obj good, adopt. This is novel.

Coordinate polish implementation:
```
def coordinate_polish(a0, budget, topm=64, q=1.0):
  a=normalize; c=self_conv(a); best_obj=...
  best_a=a.copy()
  start=now
  no_sweeps=0
  while time<budget and no_sweeps<3:
     # compute weights and scores
     w=power_weights(c, q=2.0)
     d=gradient(w,a)
     dm=d.mean(); scale=d.std()+eps
     score=np.abs(d-dm)/scale
     # include largest a and smallest a
     top_score=np.argsort(score)[-topm:]
     top_a=np.argsort(a)[-topm//4:]
     bottom_a=np.argsort(a)[:topm//4]
     idx=np.unique(np.concatenate((top_score,top_a,bottom_a)))
     improved=False
     for i in idx[::-1]: # maybe descending score
       old=a[i]
       # candidates: if old <= tiny, set to [1e-6,1e-4,mean_a/10,mean_a]
       # else multiplicative factors.
       if old < 1e-14:
          cands=[1e-8,1e-6, a.mean()*0.1, a.mean()]
       else:
          cands=[]
          for f in [0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5,2.0,4.0,0.125,8.0]: cands.append(old*f)
          # Also perturb by abs(dev) maybe: old + sign? use factors enough.
       l=i; r=i+n # affected k=i..i+n-1 inclusive; length n
       len_c=2*n-1
       if r>len_c: r=len_c # i max n-1 -> r=2n-1 ok len_c=2n-1 (exclusive? indexes 0..2n-2; r=2n-1)
       # For evaluating candidates, we can loop factors:
       best_val=old; best_objlocal=best_obj
       # vectorized affected base? idx_j = np.arange(n); but only valid length n for all i (i+n-1 <=2n-2) yes.
       j_idx = np.arange(n)  # create once outside
       c_aff_old = c[j_idx+i]
       for val in cands:
          delta=val-old
          # s=1+delta (since sum old=1)
          s=1.0+delta
          if s<=0: continue
          # raw_new = old c_aff + 2*delta*a[j_idx] ; diag add delta^2 at pos i (j_idx including i -> position i)
          raw = c_aff_old + 2.0*delta*a[j_idx]
          raw[i] += delta*delta  # because positions: j_idx[i]=i => k=i+i=2i pos i in c_aff
          m_aff=raw.max()
          # unaffected max
          if l==0:
             m_un=0.0
          else:
             m_un=c[:l].max()
          if r<len_c:
             m_un2=c[r:].max()
             if m_un2>m_un: m_un=m_un2
          m = m_aff if m_aff>m_un else m_un
          obj_candidate = (2.0*n)*m/(s*s)
          if obj_candidate < best_objlocal - 1e-14:
             best_objlocal=obj_candidate; best_val=val
       if best_val != old:
          # accept update
          delta=best_val-old; s=1+delta
          raw = c_aff_old + 2.0*delta*a[j_idx]
          raw[i] += delta*delta
          c = c.copy() # or in-place with old saved
          c[:]=? We can update in-place:
          c_copy = c.copy()
          # scale whole by 1/s^2
          scale=1/(s*s)
          c *= scale
          c[j_idx+i] = raw*scale
          a = a.copy(); a /= s; a[i]=best_val/s
          best_obj=calc? use best_objlocal
          improved=True
          # update objective maybe
     if not improved: no_sweeps+=1 else no_sweeps=0
     # recompute c from a occasionally to correct rounding
     if (sweep%5==0): c=self_conv(a)
  return best_a,best_obj
```
But copying c for each accepted coordinate O(n) okay. However candidate eval uses c and a current; after accepted, c updated. Need ensure `c_agg_old` for next coordinate reflects updated c. Good.
Potential bug: if we do `c *= scale` in-place, then the `c[:l].max()` for later unaffected uses scaled c. correct.

`raw` uses current c_aff and current a; correct.
`l=i`, `r=i+n` (exclusive). Affected indices are i..i+n-1 inclusive? k-i from 0 to n-1 => k from i to i+n-1. Exclusive end i+n. For i=n-1, end=2n-1, length_n, last index 2n-2, good. len_c=2n-1; c[r:] with r=len_c -> empty.

`raw[i]`: c[j_idx+i] array length n; position corresponding to j=i is index i (since j_idx[i]=i). Good; k=2i. If i>n-1? no. k=2i always within 0..2n-2, and k-i=i within 0..n-1. So diag present. If old sum 1. Good.
If `best_val` very negative? We only positive candidates. If old zero and val small, s=1+val.
We should ensure val not exceed 1000? p small. fine.

Potential bug in `_weights_from_c`: For q power, if q very high, w sum underflows? Use log weights:
```
def power_weights(c, q):
   cmax=c.max(); if cmax<=0: return ones
   r=c/cmax
   # logw = q*log(r+1e-300)
   logw = q*np.log(np.maximum(r,1e-300))
   logw -= logw.max()
   w=np.exp(logw)
   s=w.sum()
   if s<1e-300: top np.argmax etc
```
This avoids underflow for r small. q up to 500; log min ~q*log1e-300 if r zero (c can be zero at edges) -> -690*q huge; subtract max could be finite? If all but top zeros, logw for top 0; for r zero, log -inf; exp 0. Good. Use `np.where(r>0, q*np.log(r), -1e300)`.
But for c max, top weights. If q=1, log weights fine.
Alternative r**q simpler, but underflow only for small; finite. Use log.

Gradient from weights: If weights sum 1, d positive. If weights from log with top only, gradient sharp. Good.

Objective calc:
```
def objective_normalized(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
   if n==0: return np.inf
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-15: return np.inf
   a=a/s
   c=_self_conv(a)
   # c may have slight negative? max positive.
   return float(2.0*n*c.max())
```

Let's define `_exp_log_to_prob(loga)`:
```
def make_from_log(loga):
    loga = np.asarray(loga,float)
    loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, -700.0)
    loga -= loga.max()
    p = np.exp(loga)
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(p.size, 1.0/p.size), np.full(loga.size, np.log(1.0/p.size))
    p /= s
    # update log? not needed, but to keep consistent: logp=np.log(p)
    return p, np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
```

```
def gradient_from_weights(w,a):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    if w.size != L: 
       # if w from c, yes
    size = 1 << (3*n - 3).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w_rev,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,size)
    return 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```

def convolve_ab(a,b):
   L=len(a)+len(b)-1; size=1<<(L-1).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size); return c[:L]

```
"""
... docstring
"""
import numpy as np
import time

def _conv_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0: return np.array([0.0])
    size = 1 << (2*n - 2).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
Check n=1: 2n-2=0 bit_length0 ->size1, c[:1]. ok.
`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.full(a.size,1.0/a.size if a.size else 1.0)
    return a/s
```
`_objective`:
```
def _objective(a):
    a=_normalize(a); n=a.size
    if n==0 or n<1: return np.inf
    c=_conv_self(a)
    m=c.max()
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m<0: m=0
    return float(2.0*n*m)
```
For speed in loops, avoid re-normalizing if already normalized; make fast `_objective_f(a)` assumes normalized:
```
def _obj_norm(a):
   c=_conv_self(a); return float(2.0*len(a)*c.max())
```
And `_sum1` before.

def _power_sequence(n, alpha, beta, eps_l=1.0, eps_r=0.25):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    left=i+eps_l
    right=(n-1.0-i)+eps_r
    logp = -alpha*np.log(left) + beta*np.log(right)
    # If right eps small and beta positive, log finite. If alpha=0 left constant.
    # Add tiny floor to prevent all zeros? no.
    logp -= np.max(logp)
    p=np.exp(logp)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: p=np.exp(np.zeros(n)); s=n
    p/=s
    return p
```

def _adam_log_seq(a0, n, budget, q0=2.0, q1=80.0, lr0=0.04, lr1=0.002, verbose=False):
    a=_normalize(a0)
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=_obj_norm(a)
    start=time.monotonic(); t=0; no_imp=0
    b1=0.9; b2=0.995
    while time.monotonic() - start < budget:
        t += 1
        elapsed...
        q = q0 + (q1-q0)*progress
        a_norm = _from_log(loga)
        c = _conv_self(a_norm)
        coeff = c.max(); obj = 2*n*coeff
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
            best_obj=obj; best_a=a_norm.copy(); no_imp=0
        else: no_imp += 1
        if no_imp > 300:
            # perturbation
            rng = np.random.default_rng(t+int(best_obj*1e6)) # top-level? can't create many?
            noise = 0.01 * np.exp(-progress*2) * rng.standard_normal(n)
            # add in log? a_noise=_normalize(a_norm + noise * a_norm.mean())
            loga = np.log(np.maximum(a_norm + noise * a_norm.mean(), 1e-300))
            m.fill(0); v.fill(0)
            no_imp=0
        w=_peak_weights(c, q)
        d=_gradient_from_weights(w, a_norm)
        dot=float(np.dot(a_norm,d))
        g=a_norm*(d - dot)
        m = b1*m + (1-b1)*g
        v = b2*v + (1-b2)*g*g
        mhat = m/(1-b1**t)
        vhat = v/(1-b2**t)
        lr = lr0 * (1-progress) + lr1*progress
        # To avoid huge, scale lr by sqrt(4096/n)
        lr *= (4096.0/float(n))**0.25
        loga = loga - lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
        # center loga occasionally
        if t % 100 == 0:
            loga -= loga.mean()
        if verbose and t % 500 ==0: print
    return best_a,best_obj
```
Creating rng inside loop with default_rng each no_imp can be slow. Use a single rng seed at function start:
`rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + int(n))` top module? In function, okay; not nested closure. But no need many.
`b1**t` for t large okay. vhat can zeros; eps.

We only update best when improves; but current loga may degrade, no_imp triggers reset to best? We didn't reset loga to best. If current degrades a lot, no_imp after 300 updates with noise but loga may have wandered. We should every 100 checks if current obj > best_obj*(1+0.05): loga = log(best_a)+small noise; reset m,v. ```

def _mirror_log_seq(...):
  a=_normalize; loga=log(a); best; start; rng; step=0
  while time:
    step+=1; progress
    a=_from_log(loga); c... obj update
    if no_improve > 60: # shuffle around best
       if best_obj finite:
         a = _normalize(best_a * (1 + 0.02*rng.normal))
         loga=log(a)
    q=...
    w=_peak_weights(c,q)
    d=_gradient(w,a)
    # use weighted average mean? d positive.
    med = np.median(d) if n>1 else d[0]
    scale = max(np.percentile(d,95)-np.percentile(d,5), 1e-12, 1e-6*med if med>0 else 1e-12)
    z=(d-med)/scale
    z=np.clip(z,-6,6)
    # objective line search: current obj from c
    tried=False; eta=base_eta
    for back in range(10):
       loga_t=loga - eta*z
       a_t=_from_log(loga_t)
       c_t=_conv_self(a_t); obj_t=2*n*c_t.max()
       if obj_t < obj - 1e-15:
          loga=loga_t; a=a_t; obj=obj_t; tried=True; break
       eta*=0.5
    if not tried:
       # if current already best? maybe skip
       if obj <= best_obj*(1+1e-8):
          # small random log perturbation? or reduce eta
          pass
       else:
          loga = loga + 0.01*rng.normal(n) maybe normalized

  return
```

```
def _convex_mimic_seq(a0,n,budget,verbose=False):
    f=_normalize(a0); best_a=f.copy(); best_obj=_obj_norm(f); start=now; rng
    logg=np.log(f+1e-300)
    cycles=0; noimp=0
    while time<deadline:
       cycles+=1
       cf=self_conv(f); M=cf.max()
       if M<=0: break
       # inner iterations count based on n: maybe max(50, int(3.0 * 4096/n * 100))? Use time: run up to 3s or 100 iterations.
       inner_steps = 120
       inner_start=time
       for st in range(inner_steps):
          if time-start>budget: break
          if time-inner_start > 3.0: break
          g=_from_log(logg)
          r=_convolve_ab(f,g)
          viol=np.maximum(r - M, 0.0)
          s=viol.sum()
          if s < 1e-15:
             break
          w=viol/s
          d=_gradient_from_weights(w, f) * 0.5
          dmax=d.max()
          if dmax<=0: break
          eta=0.2
          logg = logg - eta*(d/dmax)
          # normalize log? keep bounded
       g=_from_log(logg)
       # evaluate g and mixes
       objs: obj_g=_obj_norm(g)
       candidates=[g]
       for alpha in [1,0.8,0.6,0.4,0.2,0.1]: h=(1-alpha)*f+alpha*g; h=_normalize(h); candidates append
       for h in candidates: obj=...; if improve: best; noimp=0 else noimp++
       # Update f to best? If best improved? We can set f=best_a if improved else keep f? Maybe set f = normalize(f + 0.5*g) if no improvement? To explore.
       f=_normalize(best_a) if best_improved else f
       if noimp>3: break
    return best
```

def _coordinate_polish(a0,n,budget,topm=64,verbose=False):
   a=_normalize; best_a; best_obj; start; rng; sweeps=0
   j_idx=np.arange(n)
   while time<deadline and sweeps<50:
      sweeps+=1
      # recompute exact
      c=_conv_self(a); obj=2n cmax; update best
      # weights/scores
      w=_peak_weights(c,2.0)
      d=_gradient_from_weights(w,a)
      dm=d.mean(); sd=d.std()
      score=np.abs(d-dm)/(sd+eps)
      # top indices
      idx_score=np.argsort(score)[-topm:]
      # also top a
      top_a=np.argsort(a)[-max(4,topm//4):]
      bot_a=np.argsort(a)[:max(4,topm//4)]
      idx=np.unique(np.concatenate((idx_score, top_a, bot_a)))
      improved_sweep=False
      for ii in range(len(idx)-1,-1,-1):
         i=int(idx[ii])
         old=a[i]
         # build cands
         if old <= 1e-14:
            mean=a.sum()/n  # 1/n
            cands=np.array([1e-8,1e-6,1e-4,mean*0.1,mean,mean*10])
         else:
            factors=(0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5,2.0,8.0,0.125)
            cands=old*np.array(factors)
         l=i; r=i+n; len_c=2*n-1
         aff_slice = slice(l,r)
         old_aff = c[aff_slice]
         # If old small, factors may produce val==old? ensure distinct round
         best_val=old; best_local=best_obj
         for val in cands:
            if val <= 0 or not np.isfinite(val): continue
            delta=val-old
            if delta == 0: continue
            s=1.0+delta
            if s <= 1e-12: continue
            raw = old_aff + 2.0*delta*a[j_idx]
            raw = raw.copy()? old_aff view; adding returns new. raw[i] += delta*delta
            m_aff=raw.max()
            m_un=-1e300
            if l>0: m_un=c[:l].max()
            if r<len_c:
               m_un2=c[r:].max(); if > set
            if m_un < -1e299: m_un=0
            m = m_aff if m_aff>m_un else m_un
            obj_cand = (2.0*n)*m/(s*s)
            if obj_cand < best_local - 1e-15:
               best_local=obj_cand; best_val=val
         if best_val != old:
            delta=best_val-old; s=1+delta; scale=1/(s*s)
            raw = old_aff + 2*delta*a[j_idx]
            raw = raw.copy(); raw[i]+=delta*delta
            c_new=c.copy()
            c_new *= scale
            c_new[aff_slice] = raw*scale
            a = a.copy(); a *= scale? Actually a normalized: a /= s; a[i]=val/s
            c=c_new; a=_normalize(a)? To avoid drift, don't normalize all again (would scale c incorrectly). Use exact as above. But a /= s; a[i] = best_val/s. Sum = (1-delta? previous sum 1) ? old a sum 1; a/s sum (1/s)*(1 + best_val-old) =1. good.
            best_obj=best_local improved_sweep=True
      if not improved_sweep and sweeps>2: break
   return best_a,best_obj
```

```
def _peak_weights(c, q):
    L = len(c)
    cmax = c.max()
    if cmax <= 1e-300 or L==0:
       return np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    r = c / cmax
    # avoid log(0)
    logw = np.full(L, -1e300)
    pos = r > 1e-300
    logw[pos] = q * np.log(r[pos])
    # for top where r=1, logw=0; maybe c has multiple max
    m = logw.max()
    logw -= m
    w = np.exp(logw)
    s = w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       w = np.zeros(L); top = np.argsort(c)[-min(5,L):]; w[top]=1; s=1
    else: w /= s
    return w
```

def _from_log(loga):
    loga=np.asarray(loga,float)

    p=np.exp(loga)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=1e-300: return np.full(p.size,1.0/p.size)
    p /= s
    return p
```

```
def _optimize_pipeline(a0, n, budget, verbose=False):
    a=_normalize(a0); best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=_obj_norm(a)
    start=time.monotonic()
    # function wrappers update best
    # Adam
    b=min(budget*0.4, max(10, budget*0.4))
    if b>5:
       ca,co=_adam_log_seq(a,n,b,2,80,0.05,0.002, False)
       if co<best_obj: best_a, best_obj=ca,co; a=ca
    b=min((budget*0.25), max(5, budget*0.25))
    remaining = time.monotonic()-start; if remaining >= budget*0.65: maybe skip.
    # mirror
    # convex if remaining
    # coordinate
    # final mirror small
    return best_a,best_obj
```

```python
"""
Minimize 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2 over nonnegative sequences.

The search combines several ideas:
1. A structured one-sided power-law family:
   a_i ~ (i+eps_L)^(-alpha) * (n-1-i+eps_R)^(beta)
   which explores edge-singular / edge-zero shapes and is very efficient to scan.
2. Multi-start exploitation from the provided best sequence and scanned parents.
3. Smooth log-domain optimization (Adam and mirror line-search) using exact
   weighted subgradients computed by FFT.
4. A convex LP-like update: for fixed f, iteratively reduce violations of
   (f*g) <= M to produce a useful g, then mix g into f.
5. Exact coordinate descent polish with fast O(n) affected-convolution updates.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def _from_log(loga):
    loga = np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64)
    loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, -700.0)
    loga -= loga.max()
    p = np.exp(loga)
    s = p.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(p.size, 1.0 / p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s


def _conv_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    size = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(c[:L])


def _convolve_ab(a, b):
    A = len(a)
    B = len(b)
    L = A + B - 1
    size = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, size)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(c[:L])


def _obj_norm(a):
    """Objective for a normalized nonnegative sequence (sum = 1)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_self(a)
    m = c.max()
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def _gradient_from_weights(w, a):
    """
    Exact gradient of F(a)=sum_k w_k (a*a)_k.
    w has length 2n-1. Returns d_i = 2 sum_{j valid} a_j w_{i+j}.
    """
    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if len(w) != L:
        w = np.resize(w, L)
    size = 1 << (3 * n - 3).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, size)
    return 2.0 * c[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]


def _peak_weights(c, q):
    """Weights emphasizing large convolution coefficients."""
    L = len(c)
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([1.0])
    if q <= 0.0:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = c.max()
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    r = c / cmax
    logw = np.full(L, -1e300, dtype=np.float64)
    pos = r > 1e-300
    logw[pos] = q * np.log(r[pos])
    mx = logw.max()
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    logw -= mx
    w = np.exp(logw)
    s = w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        k = min(5, L)
        idx = np.argsort(c)[-k:]
        w[idx] = 1.0
        s = float(k)
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def _power_sequence(n, alpha, beta, eps_l=1.0, eps_r=0.25):
    """
    Structured sequence
        p_i ~ (i + eps_l)^(-alpha) * (n-1-i + eps_r)^(beta).
    Positive alpha puts mass near the left edge.
    Positive beta drives the right edge toward zero.
    """
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    left = i + eps_l
    right = (n - 1.0 - i) + eps_r
    logp = -alpha * np.log(left) + beta * np.log(right)
    logp -= logp.max()
    p = np.exp(logp)
    s = p.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    else:
        p /= s
    return p


def _add_top(cands, obj, a, k=6):
    if not np.isfinite(obj):
        return cands
    cands.append([obj, a])
    idx = len(cands) - 1
    while idx > 0 and cands[idx][0] < cands[idx - 1][0]:
        cands[idx], cands[idx - 1] = cands[idx - 1], cands[idx]
        idx -= 1
    if len(cands) > k:
        cands.pop()
    return cands


def _scan_power_seeds(lengths, deadline, max_evals=450):
    """
    Fast scan of the structured family. Returns list of [obj, (n,alpha,beta,eps_r), a].
    """
    cands = []
    count = 0

    alphas = [0.0, 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20, 0.25, 0.30, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45, 0.48]
    betas = [
        -0.40, -0.25, -0.15, -0.05, 0.0,
        0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 1.00, 1.10, 1.20
    ]
    epsrs = [0.25, 0.5, 1.0]

    for n in lengths:
        done = False
        for epsr in epsrs:
            if done:
                break
            for alpha in alphas:
                if done:
                    break
                for beta in betas:
                    if count >= max_evals or time.monotonic() > deadline:
                        done = True
                        break
                    a = _power_sequence(n, alpha, beta, 1.0, epsr)
                    obj = _obj_norm(a)
                    count += 1
                    cands.append([obj, (n, alpha, beta, epsr), a])
                    # Insert into top list.
                    idx = len(cands) - 1
                    while idx > 0 and cands[idx][0] < cands[idx - 1][0]:
                        cands[idx], cands[idx - 1] = cands[idx - 1], cands[idx]
                        idx -= 1
                    if len(cands) > 8:
                        cands.pop()
    return cands


def _adam_log_seq(a0, n, budget, q0=2.0, q1=80.0, lr0=0.045, lr1=0.002):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if n != a.size:
        n = a.size
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = _obj_norm(a)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + 997 * n)
    b1 = 0.9
    b2 = 0.995
    start = time.monotonic()
    t = 0
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < budget:
        t += 1
        progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1.0, budget))
        q = q0 + (q1 - q0) * progress

        a = _from_log(loga)
        c = _conv_self(a)
        obj = _obj_norm(a)

        if not np.isfinite(obj) or obj > best_obj * 1.05 + 1e-8:
            if time.monotonic() - start > 5.0:
                noise = 0.02 * max(0.1, 1.0 - progress)
                aa = _normalize(best_a + noise * best_a.mean() * rng.standard_normal(n))
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(aa, 1e-300))
                m.fill(0.0)
                v.fill(0.0)
                no_improve = 0
                continue
        else:
            if obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
                best_obj = obj
                best_a = a.copy()
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 350:
            noise = 0.02 * max(0.1, 1.0 - progress)
            aa = _normalize(best_a + noise * best_a.mean() * rng.standard_normal(n))
            loga = np.log(np.maximum(aa, 1e-300))
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        w = _peak_weights(c, q)
        d = _gradient_from_weights(w, a)
        dot = float(np.dot(a, d))
        g = a * (d - dot)

        if not np.isfinite(g).all() or np.max(np.abs(g)) <= 1e-22:
            break

        m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * g
        v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * (g * g)
        mhat = m / (1.0 - b1 ** t)
        vhat = v / (1.0 - b2 ** t)

        lr = (lr0 * (1.0 - progress) + lr1 * progress) * (4096.0 / float(n)) ** 0.25
        loga = loga - lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)

        if t % 100 == 0:
            if np.isfinite(loga).all():
                loga -= loga.mean()

    a = _from_log(loga)
    obj = _obj_norm(a)
    if obj < best_obj:
        return a, obj
    return best_a, best_obj


def _mirror_log_seq(a0, n, budget, q0=1.0, q1=40.0, eta0=0.18, eta1=0.005):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if n != a.size:
        n = a.size
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = _obj_norm(a)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(987654 + 7919 * n)
    start = time.monotonic()
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < budget:
        progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1.0, budget))
        q = q0 + (q1 - q0) * progress

        a = _from_log(loga)
        c = _conv_self(a)
        obj = _obj_norm(a)

        if not np.isfinite(obj) or obj > best_obj * 1.08 + 1e-8:
            if time.monotonic() - start > 5.0:
                noise = 0.02 * max(0.1, 1.0 - progress)
                aa = _normalize(best_a + noise * best_a.mean() * rng.standard_normal(n))
                a = aa
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(aa, 1e-300))
                no_improve = 0
                continue
        else:
            if obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
                best_obj = obj
                best_a = a.copy()
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1

        w = _peak_weights(c, q)
        d = _gradient_from_weights(w, a)
        if not np.isfinite(d).all():
            break

        med = float(np.median(d)) if n > 1 else float(d[0])
        p95 = float(np.percentile(d, 95))
        p05 = float(np.percentile(d, 5))
        scale = max(p95 - p05, 1e-9 * max(1.0, abs(med)), 1e-300)
        z = (d - med) / scale
        z = np.clip(z, -6.0, 6.0)
        if np.max(np.abs(z)) <= 1e-12:
            no_improve += 5
            if no_improve > 30:
                noise = 0.015 * max(0.1, 1.0 - progress)
                aa = _normalize(best_a + noise * best_a.mean() * rng.standard_normal(n))
                a = aa
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(aa, 1e-300))
                no_improve = 0
            continue

        eta = (eta0 * (1.0 - progress) + eta1 * progress) * (4096.0 / float(n)) ** 0.25
        accepted = False
        for _ in range(12):
            log_trial = loga - eta * z
            a_trial = _from_log(log_trial)
            c_trial = _conv_self(a_trial)
            obj_trial = float(2.0 * n * c_trial.max())
            if np.isfinite(obj_trial) and obj_trial < obj - 1e-15:
                loga = log_trial
                a = a_trial
                obj = obj_trial
                accepted = True
                no_improve = 0
                if obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = obj
                    best_a = a.copy()
                break
            eta *= 0.5

        if not accepted:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 40:
                noise = 0.018 * max(0.1, 1.0 - progress)
                aa = _normalize(best_a + noise * best_a.mean() * rng.standard_normal(n))
                a = aa
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(aa, 1e-300))
                no_improve = 0

    obj = _obj_norm(a)
    if obj < best_obj:
        return a, obj
    return best_a, best_obj


def _convex_mimic_seq(a0, n, budget):
    """
    For fixed f, try to find g by reducing violations of (f*g) <= M.
    Then mix g into f. This approximates the LP update from the SOTA method.
    """
    f = _normalize(a0)
    if n != f.size:
        n = f.size
    best_a = f.copy()
    best_obj = _obj_norm(f)

    logg = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1e-300))
    start = time.monotonic()
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < budget:
        cf = _conv_self(f)
        M = cf.max() * 0.99
        if M <= 1e-300:
            break

        inner_deadline = min(time.monotonic() + 2.5, start + budget)
        for _ in range(180):
            if time.monotonic() > inner_deadline:
                break
            g = _from_log(logg)
            r = _convolve_ab(f, g)
            viol = np.maximum(r - M, 0.0)
            s = viol.sum()
            if s <= 1e-15:
                break
            w = viol / s
            d = 0.5 * _gradient_from_weights(w, f)
            dm = d.max()
            if dm <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(dm):
                break
            eta = 0.22
            logg = logg - eta * (d / dm)
            if not np.isfinite(logg).all():
                logg = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1e-300))
                break

        g = _from_log(logg)
        improved = False

        gobj = _obj_norm(g)
        if gobj < best_obj - 1e-14:
            best_obj = gobj
            best_a = g.copy()
            improved = True

        alphas = [1.0, 0.8, 0.6, 0.4, 0.25, 0.1, 0.5]
        for al in alphas:
            h = _normalize((1.0 - al) * f + al * g)
            objh = _obj_norm(h)
            if objh < best_obj - 1e-14:
                best_obj = objh
                best_a = h.copy()
                improved = True

        if improved:
            f = best_a.copy()
            logg = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1e-300))
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 3:
                break

    obj = _obj_norm(f)
    if obj < best_obj:
        return f, obj
    return best_a, best_obj


def _coordinate_polish(a0, n, budget, topm=72):
    """
    Exact coordinate polish.
    For one coordinate i, changing a_i to val changes only convolution
    coefficients k=i..i+n-1. Candidate max can be evaluated with two maxima
    instead of a full FFT.
    """
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if n != a.size:
        n = a.size
    if budget < 8.0:
        return a, _obj_norm(a)

    c = _conv_self(a)
    cur_obj = _obj_norm(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = cur_obj
    j_idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    start = time.monotonic()
    sweep = 0
    factors = (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0, 0.125, 8.0)

    while time.monotonic() - start < budget and sweep < 60:
        sweep += 1
        c = _conv_self(a)
        cur_obj = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
        if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
            best_obj = cur_obj
            best_a = a.copy()

        w = _peak_weights(c, 1.5)
        d = _gradient_from_weights(w, a)
        dm = float(d.mean()) if d.size else 0.0
        sd = float(d.std()) if d.size else 0.0
        score = np.abs(d - dm) / max(sd, 1e-18)
        score = np.where(np.isfinite(score), score, 0.0)

        top_score = np.argsort(score)[-topm:]
        k2 = max(4, topm // 4)
        top_big = np.argsort(a)[-k2:]
        top_small = np.argsort(a)[:k2]
        idx = np.unique(np.concatenate((top_score, top_big, top_small)))

        improved_this_sweep = False

        for ii in range(len(idx) - 1, -1, -1):
            i = int(idx[ii])
            old = float(a[i])

            if old <= 1e-14:
                avg = 1.0 / float(n)
                cand_vals = np.array([1e-10, 1e-8, 1e-6, 1e-4, avg * 0.1, avg, avg * 10.0], dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                cand_vals = old * np.array(factors, dtype=np.float64)

            l = i
            r = i + n
            len_c = 2 * n - 1
            aff = slice(l, r)

            old_aff = c[aff]
            best_val = old
            best_local = cur_obj

            for val in cand_vals:
                if not np.isfinite(val) or val <= 0.0:
                    continue
                delta = float(val - old)
                if abs(delta) <= 1e-18:
                    continue
                s = 1.0 + delta
                if s <= 1e-300:
                    continue

                raw = old_aff + 2.0 * delta * a[j_idx]
                raw = raw.copy()
                raw[i] += delta * delta

                m_aff = float(raw.max()) if raw.size else 0.0
                m_un = -1e300
                if l > 0:
                    m_un = max(m_un, float(c[:l].max()))
                if r < len_c:
                    m_un = max(m_un, float(c[r:].max()))
                if m_un <= -1e299:
                    m_un = 0.0

                m = m_aff if m_aff > m_un else m_un
                cand_obj = (2.0 * n) * m / (s * s)
                if cand_obj < best_local - 1e-15:
                    best_local = cand_obj
                    best_val = float(val)

            if abs(best_val - old) > 1e-18:
                delta = float(best_val - old)
                s = 1.0 + delta
                scale = 1.0 / (s * s)

                raw = old_aff + 2.0 * delta * a[j_idx]
                raw = raw.copy()
                raw[i] += delta * delta

                c_new = c.copy()
                c_new *= scale
                c_new[aff] = raw * scale
                c = c_new

                a = a.copy()
                a /= s
                a[i] = float(best_val) / s
                a = _normalize(a)  # protect accumulated rounding

                cur_obj = best_local
                improved_this_sweep = True
                if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = a.copy()

        if not improved_this_sweep and sweep >= 3:
            break

    return best_a, best_obj


def _optimize_pipeline(a0, n, budget):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if n != a.size:
        n = a.size
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = _obj_norm(a)
    start = time.monotonic()

    # Adam in log domain.
    if budget > 25.0:
        t1 = min(0.42 * budget, max(15.0, budget - 30.0))
        ca, obj_a = _adam_log_seq(a, n, t1, q0=2.0, q1=90.0, lr0=0.05, lr1=0.002)
        obj_a = _obj_norm(ca)
        if obj_a < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj_a
            best_a = ca.copy()
            a = ca.copy()

    # Mirror descent with line search.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    if budget - elapsed > 20.0:
        t2 = min(0.27 * budget, max(10.0, budget - elapsed - 15.0))
        cm, obj_m = _mirror_log_seq(a, n, t2, q0=1.0, q1=45.0, eta0=0.18, eta1=0.004)
        obj_m = _obj_norm(cm)
        if obj_m < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj_m
            best_a = cm.copy()
            a = cm.copy()

    # Convex LP-like update.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    if budget - elapsed > 25.0:
        t3 = min(0.12 * budget, max(8.0, budget - elapsed - 10.0))
        cc, obj_c = _convex_mimic_seq(a, n, t3)
        obj_c = _obj_norm(cc)
        if obj_c < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj_c
            best_a = cc.copy()
            a = cc.copy()

    # Coordinate polish.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    if budget - elapsed > 20.0:
        t4 = max(10.0, budget - elapsed - 5.0)
        if n > 8192:
            topm = 40
        elif n > 4096:
            topm = 60
        else:
            topm = 84
        cp, obj_p = _coordinate_polish(a, n, t4, topm=topm)
        obj_p = _obj_norm(cp)
        if obj_p < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj_p
            best_a = cp.copy()
            a = cp.copy()

    # Very short final mirror on the best.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    if budget - elapsed > 15.0:
        t5 = max(5.0, budget - elapsed - 2.0)
        cf, obj_f = _mirror_log_seq(best_a, n, t5, q0=3.0, q1=120.0, eta0=0.08, eta1=0.002)
        obj_f = _obj_norm(cf)
        if obj_f < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj_f
            best_a = cf.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2380.0, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2380.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s < 30.0:
        budget_s = 2380.0

    # Leave room for final verification and意外 overhead.
    total = min(max(budget_s, 30.0), 2395.0)
    work_budget = max(20.0, total - 25.0)
    now = time.monotonic()
    deadline = now + work_budget

    global_a = None
    global_obj = np.inf

    def _consider(a):
        nonlocal global_a, global_obj
        if a is None:
            return
        a = _normalize(a)
        if a.size == 0:
            return
        obj = _obj_norm(a)
        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < global_obj:
            global_obj = obj
            global_a = a.copy()

    # Load the provided construction, if available.
    given = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if given is not None:
        try:
            g = np.asarray(given, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if g.size >= 8 and g.sum() > 0.01:
                g = _normalize(g)
                if g.size <= 16384:
                    print("Given seed: n=%d obj=%.7f" % (g.size, _obj_norm(g)))
                    _consider(g)
        except Exception as e:
            print("Could not use given sequence: %s" % str(e))

    # Prepare candidate lengths.
    lengths = []
    if global_a is not None and global_a.size <= 8192:
        lengths.append(global_a.size)
    for n in (4096, 8192):
        if n not in lengths:
            lengths.append(n)
    if global_a is not None and global_a.size > 8192 and global_a.size <= 16384:
        lengths.append(global_a.size)

    # Structured scan.
    scan_budget = min(40.0, max(8.0, 0.04 * work_budget))
    scan_deadline = min(time.monotonic() + scan_budget, deadline - 10.0)
    try:
        scan_top = _scan_power_seeds(lengths, scan_deadline, max_evals=420)
        for item in scan_top:
            a = item[2]
            if a is not None:
                _consider(a)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Scan failed: %s" % str(e))

    # Build a small list of strong starts.
    starts = []
    if global_a is not None:
        starts = _add_top(starts, _obj_norm(global_a), global_a.copy(), 6)

    if scan_top:
        for item in scan_top[:6]:
            obj = item[0]
            a = item[2]
            if a is not None:
                starts = _add_top(starts, obj, a, 6)

    # De-duplicate starts loosely by first 16 values and length.
    unique_starts = []
    for st in starts:
        a = st[1]
        sig = (a.size, tuple(a[:16].round(12)), tuple(a[-8:].round(12)))
        append = True
        for u in unique_starts:
            if u[1] == sig:
                append = False
                break
        if append:
            unique_starts.append([st[0], sig, a])
    unique_starts.sort()
    start_a = [u[2] for u in unique_starts[:4]]

    # Main optimization rounds.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - now
    opt_budget = max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0)
    fractions = [0.52, 0.22, 0.12, 0.14]

    for k, sa in enumerate(start_a[:4]):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0:
            break
        if opt_budget <= 25.0:
            break
        if k < len(fractions):
            b = max(15.0, fractions[k] * opt_budget)
        else:
            b = max(10.0, 0.05 * opt_budget)
        b = min(b, max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 8.0))
        if b < 10.0:
            break

        n = sa.size
        print("Optimize start %d: n=%d start_obj=%.7f budget=%.1fs" %
              (k, n, _obj_norm(sa), b))
        try:
            oa, obj_new = _optimize_pipeline(sa, n, b)
        except Exception as e:
            print("Optimize pipeline failed: %s" % str(e))
            oa = sa
            obj_new = _obj_norm(sa)
        _consider(oa)
        print("  after pipeline: n=%d obj=%.7f" % (oa.size, _obj_norm(oa)))

    # Final dedicated polish on the absolute best.
    if global_a is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
        last_budget = max(5.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0)
        if last_budget > 30.0:
            n = global_a.size
            topm = 48 if n > 8192 else 80
            try:
                fa, fobj = _coordinate_polish(global_a, n, min(last_budget, 0.55 * (1 + n / 4096.0)), topm=topm)
                fobj = _obj_norm(fa)
                if fobj < global_obj:
                    global_obj = fobj
                    global_a = fa
                    print("Final coordinate polish: n=%d obj=%.7f" % (n, fobj))
            except Exception as e:
                print("Final coordinate polish failed: %s" % str(e))

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 12.0 and global_a is not None:
            n = global_a.size
            b = max(5.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 3.0)
            if b > 12.0:
                try:
                    fa, fobj = _mirror_log_seq(global_a, n, b, q0=4.0, q1=150.0, eta0=0.06, eta1=0.0015)
                    fobj = _obj_norm(fa)
                    if fobj < global_obj:
                        global_obj = fobj
                        global_a = fa
                        print("Final mirror polish: n=%d obj=%.7f" % (n, fobj))
                except Exception as e:
                    print("Final mirror polish failed: %s" % str(e))

    # Fallbacks.
    if global_a is None:
        try:
            global_a = _power_sequence(4096, 0.25, 0.45, 1.0, 0.25)
        except Exception:
            global_a = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64)

    final = np.asarray(global_a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    final = np.clip(final, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if final.size == 0 or final.sum() < 0.01:
        final = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64)

    # Verify with the official evaluator. Avoid O(n^2) calls except final checks.
    try:
        official = evaluate_sequence(final.tolist())
    except Exception:
        official = np.inf

    if not np.isfinite(official):
        fallback = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64)
        try:
            official_fb = evaluate_sequence(fallback.tolist())
        except Exception:
            official_fb = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(official_fb):
            final = fallback

    print("FINAL internal obj=%.7f n=%d" % (global_obj if np.isfinite(global_obj) else float("inf"), len(final)))
    return final.tolist()
```
